# Engine C2b: plans and blocks — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

> **Drafted 2026-10-19, revised the same day, approved by the PI on 2026-10-19, rebuilt on 2026-10-20, and given its first code task that day.** Drafted from the scope rebuilt that morning (`.superpowers/c2b-plan/scope.md`: 53 requirements, 22 binding decisions, 11 questions, 25 engineering calls), the code map at `da22ee2` (`code-map.md`, 16 surprises), the neighbors read from source (`neighbors.md`: wl-preproc `b74d4f3`, wl-xtasks `0486476`, wl-works `c13e1889`) and the PI's twelve answers of 2026-10-19. **Revised** for its engineering review and its science and animal-training review (`.superpowers/c2b-plan/reviews/`), every finding fixed in the task that owns its code with a test that fails without the fix ("The reviews' findings", below), and for **the PI's four further answers of 2026-10-19** (batch 4, from the science review's questions), which it builds as given; **the PI's seventeen answers** are re-asked nowhere ("The PI's decisions it builds"). **Approved by the PI, 2026-10-19**: Appendix A's items 1 and 2 ("Approve" each), Appendix B "Confirm all as built", and to be built subagent-driven (`answers.md`). **And his answer of 2026-10-20, "Both, polar first"** (decision 17): a setting's value that is a polar position is recorded as its eccentricity and direction with its (x, y) beside them, fixed as C2b's first code change (Task 2), wl-preproc told the shape first (Task 1). **Rebuilt on `main` at `e988553`** (2026-10-20), after two branches merged: `true-angle-windows` -- positions written as eccentricity and direction (`task.Polar`, `position_of`), arrays on a true circle, gaze windows and their distances in true visual angle, the `computed-position` warning, center scale retired -- and `saccade-fixtures-v1`. C2b's code went in around them unchanged in behavior; where it touches a position, a condition's value that is one is shown eccentricity and direction first in the review report's plan tables (Task 11), and is held to its setting's choices at load as any choice is (Task 6). The rebuild found that a run whose setting holds a `Polar` could not be recorded at all -- older than C2b -- and the PI's answer of 2026-10-20 made the fix this build's first code change (Task 2). Build C2 split in two on 2026-10-11: C2a, counting and repeats (merged at `da22ee2`), and **C2b, plans and blocks** (this plan). **Welfare**: one welfare-listed function changes, `Service._start` (three lines, its diff in Task 12), and `Session._command` gains two branches beside the pause, outside its listed parts (Appendix A, items 1 and 2, approved). **Backlog numbers**: this plan takes **XC-404 to XC-406** (XC-404 in Task 1, XC-405 in Task 5, XC-406 in Task 7, each raising "Next free"; XC-396 to XC-403 were taken on `main` while it was revised); if `main`'s Next free has moved when the build starts, the controller renumbers. Decisions TSK-57 to TSK-63 are this plan's.
>
> **What was run.** **Every task was carried out verbatim from this text** in a fresh detached scratch worktree of `main` at `e988553` (`.superpowers/c2b-plan/dryrun4`), `wl-preproc` linked as `engine-a2` links it, `engine-a2`'s interpreter, `WLX_REQUIRE_PREPROC=1 WLX_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1`: the base read **3990 passed**; this plan was committed at its path first (the controller's step); then, task by task, each Step 1's edits applied by a script reading them out of this file (every anchor once), its tests run and seen failing as Step 2 says, Steps 3 and 4's edits applied the same way, the task's tests and the whole suite run, the commit step's commands run as written (the worktree clean after each), and each break under "Prove they can fail" applied alone, its test seen failing as `N failed`, the file restored from git. **How the text was made**: the code was written and tested in a development worktree task by task -- the drafter's chain rebased onto `ff1fad6`, each review finding fixed in its owning task, then that chain rebased onto `e988553`, its conflicts resolved keeping both sides, and Task 2 put in after Task 1 -- each task's edits generated from that chain, every anchor checked to occur once as the edits before it leave the file; then the plan was carried out from this text from `e988553` as above, ending in a tree identical to the one written (the plan file aside). The counts in each task are what printed there; the whole suite after each task: the plan committed 3990 passed; Task 1 3990 passed; Task 2 3992 passed; Task 3 4006 passed; Task 4 4012 passed; Task 5 4049 passed; Task 6 4085 passed; Task 7 4102 passed; Task 8 4117 passed; Task 9 4127 passed; Task 10 4137 passed; Task 11 4140 passed. **Placeholders read in the dry run**: `YYYY-MM-DD` as 2026-10-19. **Not run**: the CI sweep (Task 12, Step 4), the CHECKPOINT entry and its commit (Task 12, Steps 6 and 7, the controller's), and Python 3.11, which this machine does not have (the engineering review ran the drafted tree under 3.11 offline and found no incompatibility).
>
> Execution: subagent-driven, a fresh implementer and reviewer per task, then a whole-branch review on the most capable model, as C1 and C2a were. **Every commit** ends with the session's attribution lines, as the repository's history does.

**Goal:** A task declares its plans in its task object and the operator chooses one as a run starts -- the page preselecting the first, the plain run kept as a choice, `wlx run --plan` at the terminal -- and the run plays it: each condition its own trial structure and that structure's counting table, its blocks in the plan's order (fixed, cyclic, balanced in shuffled rounds never back-to-back, random, or a training progression that moves up, falls back on first answers and keeps its top stage), each block ending on its targets and, as declared, a criterion, a number of trials or a time limit, the run on its plan's end or its time limit -- time read between trials on the time trials ran, pauses not counting, a drummed trial finished first, and never as the welfare stop -- a plan that cannot go on ending its run with a sentence, and a person able to move the run on a block or back one; the load-time checks refuse what cannot run and warn of what may not do what was meant; the console says each stage change and a top stage passed; the review report shows each plan in tables; and the record names the plan as declared, how and where each block ended, why a completed run ended, and which structure each trial played -- and writes a setting's polar value as its eccentricity and direction with its (x, y) beside them.

**Architecture:** `wl_xcon/record.py` writes a setting's `Polar` value as its eccentricity and direction with its (x, y) beside them (`_written`), wherever a value is written. `wl_xcon/task.py` gains the plan vocabulary -- `Block`, `Condition` and the condition orders moved from `scheduler.py` (which imports them under their old names), `Condition.structure`, `Block.trials` and `time_limit_s`, the block orders `Fixed`, `Cyclic`, `Balanced`, `Random`, `ByCriterion`, `Plan`, `Task.plans`, and `NO_LIMIT` (XC-380). `wl_xcon/scheduler.py` counts each condition by its structure's table (`countings`) and plays a plan's order: a block's place in the run's sequence (a balanced round never starting with the type just played), `ends` (why the open block ends now: its rule, a fall-back judged on each trial's first answer, its time), `following`, `advance(reason)`, `close` (how each block ended, `BlockEnd`, with its place, its number in the stream and a top stage's pass; what a timed-out block owed, `TIME` or `RUN_TIME`), `move` (a person's next or back, which starts the progress check afresh), `stalled` and `drawable` (the progress check), `because` (a block end in words with its numbers) and `drum_waiting`. `wl_xcon/check.py` adds `_plan_faults`. `wl_xcon/taskd.py` plays the run's `Plan` (`_plan`), each trial from its condition's structure, a live edit over a condition's value (its feed line naming the conditions overridden), the block's and the run's time limits on `_elapsed` between trials, each waiting for a drummed trial, the progress check in place of the counted spin guard, a feed line per plan advance, `Session._move` for two new commands, and the start and end rows' new fields (`plan`, `plan_form`, `block_ends`, `end_reason`). `wl_xcon/link.py` carries `CheckRun.plan`, `StartRun.plan`, `NextBlock`, `BackBlock`, `Preflight.plan`/`plans` and telemetry schema 17; `serve.py` the page's bodies; `service.py` the plan into the pre-flight and the run (`_start`, welfare-listed: three lines); `preflight.py` a plan the task lacks failing the task checks, the starting values narrowed to the plan's structures, each condition's values held to their ranges; `cli.py` `wlx run --plan`; `web.py` the plan picker, the moves, the plan's row and what a block past its targets draws until; `review.py` the plan in tables.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.11-3.13; dataclasses; pytest; Playwright for the page's three new browser tests (as `tests/test_page_browser.py` already runs). **No new dependency.**

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-engine-design.md` (approved 2026-10-07) §13 items 1, 3, 5 and 8 (2, 4 and 6 to 9 are later builds'), §14 item 7 (its console part is C3's), §18.1's "the plan in tables"; `docs/references/decisions.md` TSK-19, TSK-23, TSK-25, TSK-26, TSK-28, TSK-32, TSK-50, TSK-51, TSK-56; the session-levels spec §2 and §4 (the vocabulary; `BLOCK_START`/`BLOCK_END`); backlog XC-207 (closed), XC-380, XC-381, XC-386, XC-388 (closed), XC-197 (re-pointed), XC-404 to XC-406 (filed). The scope: `.superpowers/c2b-plan/scope.md` (C2a's `c2-scope.md` is lost; scope §7 risk 1), its requirements R1-R53, every file:line re-read at `da22ee2`, the base moved to `e988553` (true visual angle merged).

## The PI's decisions it builds (binding)

**His seventeen answers**, asked in the question box -- twelve from scope §3 and four from this plan's science review on 2026-10-19, one from its rebuild on 2026-10-20 -- each the option he chose, quoted (`answers.md`):

1. **Q1, a block's time runs out mid-trial: "Finish trial, then end"** -- "The trial finishes and pays if earned; the block ends at the next pause between trials. Trials it still owed are recorded as not run, 'time ran out'. It overruns by one trial at most. This is how every other stop already works." Built: Task 5 (`Scheduler.expire`, `ends`, `close`, `TIME`), Task 7 (read in the loop). TSK-59. (A drummed trial waiting finishes first, answer 2, so a block's time can run past "one trial" while it comes back: Appendix A, item 4.)
2. **Q2, time runs out while a drummed trial is coming back: "Finish the drum first"** -- "Same as your ruling for a percentage met on a wrong drummed answer: 'Training ends on a success.' The console's warning after ten failed tries, the trainer's switch, and the out-of-cage limit still apply." Built: Task 5 (`ends` is `None` while a drum waits). TSK-59.
3. **Q3, move up / move back in a training plan: "Same, but top keeps going"** -- move up "when the stage's own rule is met (e.g. 80% right over the last 20 answers, every condition shown its minimum)", "Move back one stage, starting it fresh, when a second rule the plan states is met (e.g. under 50% over the last 20), judged once that many answers are in", "but at the top stage the animal keeps working there until a time limit, a stop or the end of the session". Both rules judge answers only (his ruling of 2026-10-18). Built: Task 3 (`ByCriterion`), Task 5. TSK-57.
4. **Q4, a timed stage ends without a pass: "Move on anyway"** -- "Time-boxed stages: go to the next stage whether or not the animal passed." Built: Task 5. TSK-57.
5. **Q5, a run time limit in the plan: "Yes, in the plan"** -- "Written in the task's plan, shown in the review report; the run ends at the first pause between trials after its time. The console's scheduled stop stays as it is; whichever comes first ends the run." Built: Task 3 (`Plan.time_limit_s`), Task 7, Task 11. TSK-59.
6. **Q6, paused time: "Pauses don't count"** -- "The block's clock runs only while trials run, so a limit means minutes of work. The welfare-listed pause code stays untouched." Built: Task 7 (`_elapsed`, which a pause does not move; `_hold` untouched). TSK-59.
7. **Q7, a plan that cannot pick another trial: "End run with a sentence"** -- "The run ends cleanly, saying why in plain words; the session stays open so the operator can start another run at once. Recorded as the run's end, not a crash." Built: Task 5 (`stalled`, `drawable`), Task 7 (`end_reason`). TSK-60.
8. **Q8, balanced: "Shuffled rounds"** -- "Each round shows every block type once, in a seeded random order. If the count isn't whole rounds, a warning at load says one type runs once more (chosen by the seed) and the run goes ahead; a run cut short leaves its last round incomplete, recorded." Built: Task 3 (`Balanced`), Task 5, Task 6 (`unbalanced-plan`). TSK-58.
9. **Q8b, a plain random block order beside balanced: "Both, as two choices"** -- "A plan picks 'balanced' or 'random'; random can repeat a type and leave another out by chance, recorded with its seed." Built: Task 3 (`Random`), Task 5. TSK-58.
10. **Q9, the plan picker: "Preselect first, keep plain"** -- "The task's first plan is preselected; the plain run stays as one more labeled choice. One click to start; a wrong plan shows in the pre-flight and the run's start record." Built: Task 8 (`Preflight.plans`), Task 10. TSK-63.
11. **Q10, moving a block by hand: "'Next' and 'back one'"** -- "Both directions by hand, each recorded with who; two new commands." Each passes through `Session._command` beside the pause, outside its welfare-listed parts (Appendix A, item 2). Built: Task 5 (`Scheduler.move`), Task 9, Task 10. TSK-62.
12. **Q11, condition numbers in the recording: "Wait for the registry"** -- "Names only until C5; numbers that change meaning between task versions inside recordings are worse than none." Built: nothing strobed; XC-197 re-pointed to C5 (Task 7). TSK-61.
13. **Drummed tries toward moving back: "First answer only"** -- "Only each trial's first answer counts toward moving back, so one stubborn trial can't demote a stage the animal is otherwise doing well in." Built: Task 5 (`Scheduler.record`: the fall-back window judges each trial's first answer, whatever the stage's own rule is and however it counts drummed tries). TSK-57.
14. **Broken holds toward moving back, in hold- and fixation-training stages: "Yes, the same"** -- "A hold-training stage judges holds both ways: broken holds count toward moving up and toward moving back." Built: Task 5 (a break the stage's own table scores goes into both windows). TSK-57.
15. **A run's time limit and a drummed trial: "Finish the drum first"** -- "Same as a block's limit: 'Training ends on a success.' Bounded by the ten-tries warning, the trainer's switch and the out-of-cage limit." Built: Task 7 (the run's limit read only while no drummed trial waits). TSK-59.
16. **Balanced, back-to-back where rounds meet: "Never back-to-back"** -- "Each new round starts with a different type than the last one played, so every block boundary is a real switch; still balanced in counts." Built: Task 5 (`_type_at`: a round that would start with the type just played is turned by one). TSK-58.
17. **Recording a setting's `Polar` value (2026-10-20): "Both, polar first"** -- "Recorded as eccentricity and direction with (x, y) beside, as you set for the record; wl-preproc told the shape before it is written. Fixed inside C2b's build, which rewrites these rows anyway, as its first code change." Asked when the rebuild on the true-angle merge found a run whose setting holds a `task.Polar` faulting as its start row was written. Built: Task 2 (`record._written`), Task 1 (the ask). REC-25.

**The controller's readings of them** (`answers.md`), each built and each in Appendix B where a choice is left: Q3 + Q4 at the top stage -- the top stage keeps going until a time limit, a stop or the session's end, and its own time limit, with nowhere to move on to, ends the run (B2); Q1 + Q2 -- a block's time limit is read between trials, a drummed trial waiting finishes first, and what the block still owes is recorded as not run, "time ran out"; Q5 + Q6 -- a run's and a block's time limits are measured in time trials run (pauses excluded), read between trials, neither a welfare limit, and neither ends with stop kind `limit` (the out-of-cage welfare stop's).

**Decided before, binding on C2b** (scope §2, each quoted there with its source):

18. **Plans in the task file, chosen at run start** (2026-10-07): "beside its trial(s), one reviewable file; the operator chooses at run start among the plans it declares." Task 3, Task 8.
19. **Block order within a run, all four** (2026-10-07): "a fixed sequence; repeating (Bt1, Bt2, Bt1, Bt2...); randomized from a recorded seed, balanced; progression by criterion (training stages, shaping)." Task 5.
20. **What ends a block, all four** (2026-10-07): "a number of trials; every condition's target met; a performance criterion; a time limit." Task 5, Task 7.
21. **What ends a run** (2026-10-07): "its plan done; an operator stop; a time limit. Disengagement ... was offered and not chosen." Task 7.
22. **Several structures, chosen per condition** (2026-10-07): "a task declares several trial structures; each condition names its own; interleaving them in a block is ordinary." Task 4.
23. **Training progressions** (2026-10-07): "Both ways, by criteria": "advance on one criterion, fall back on another, as declared." Task 5 (with Q3 and answers 13 and 14).
24. **"Balanced" within each run** (2026-10-11). Task 5 (with Q8 and answer 16).
25. **Warnings over refusals** (2026-10-11): "warnings are more appropriate most of the time with an explanation of why it may not be a good idea ... the experimenter needs free control most of the time." Task 6.
26. **The vocabulary** (2026-10-01): a block type is a set of conditions, "it is important that blocks are temporal instances"; a block lies inside one run. Task 3, Task 7.
27. **No day's plan from wl.works** (2026-10-01): "block plans and conditions come from the task programs (wl-xtasks) or are chosen at the rig". Task 3 (XC-207 closed, Task 7).
28. **Repeats in every block, time-ended ones included** (2026-10-11, 2026-10-12): a repeat "goes at a random slot within the next k trials", k "At least 3". Unchanged (C2a's), and timed blocks keep it.
29. **How a criterion block ends** (2026-10-18): "Both: criterion and every target"; "Finish the drum first"; only answers judged; a hold- or fixation-training block's scored break, "Yes, where the block says so". Unchanged, and a number of trials keeps targets a minimum the same way (Task 5).
30. **Whose repeat limit** (2026-10-18): "No: the task's limit applies unless the block sets its own" -- and so XC-380's `NO_LIMIT` (Task 3).
31. **What "trials" counts** (2026-10-18): "200 trials; drummed repeats inside them". A block's number of trials counts so (Task 5).
32. **The scheduled stop** (2026-10-18): "Keep counting presentations". `_schedule` untouched; whichever of it and a plan's time limit comes first ends the run (Q5).
33. **Training aids** (2026-10-18): off by default; outside a training session "Allow it, with a warning". A block declaring drumming is how it is on from a run's first trial (Appendix A, item 5).
34. **The one welfare duration limit** (2026-09-19; 8 h since 2026-10-01): out of the cage to back in it; "no session-length or trial maximum". A plan's time limit is design, never welfare (Appendix A, item 4).
35. **Build C split** (2026-10-11): C3 varying conditions and the live-edit layer; C6 programs, rig edits and interludes; C8 the review report's census. "Not in C2b".
36. **The format's agreement** (2026-10-11): the format is defined in wl-xcon, "his approval of build C's plan is the agreement". Task 1 tells wl-xtasks.
37. **`CONDITION` waits for conditions** (2026-09-30) -- now for the registry (Q11).
38. **Operator edits at the rig** (2026-10-07): "Edit freely ... refused if not" -- C3's; the picker leaves room for it.
39. **The welfare list is not widened by a build** (2026-09-30, 2026-10-02), and a docstring change inside a listed function goes to the PI (2026-10-01). Appendix A.

## Calls

Engineering decisions made without asking, each with what it costs if wrong. The scope's §4 calls are adopted unless the code map or an answer contradicts them, said so where they do.

1. **The plan vocabulary lives in `task.py`** (scope call 1): `Order`, `Shuffled`, `WithReplacement`, `Constrained`, `Condition` and `Block` move from `scheduler.py`, which imports them under the same names, so every `from wl_xcon.scheduler import Block` keeps working and the format pins (`tests/test_declarations.py`'s `_vocabulary`, by module) read them. The named tables (`CORRECT_ONLY`, `PRESENTED`, `EVERY_TRIAL`) stay in `scheduler.py`. Every field is added at a default, so no task's hash moves and `FORMAT` stays 1 (Task 3). Cost if wrong: imports move later.
2. **`canonical` does not learn sets** (scope call 2, **not adopted**): the code map's probe shows `canonical` already hashes a `Block`, and no plan field is a set; a set-typed field added later would need it. Cost: none now.
3. **`Task.plans: dict[str, Plan]`, empty by default** (scope call 3): a task with none runs only the plain run. Cost: a required default plan would be a load-time rule later.
4. **A block's end rules** (scope call 4): its targets, and as declared a criterion, **a number of trials** (`Block.trials`) and **a time limit** (`Block.time_limit_s`). With a criterion or a number of trials **its targets are a minimum**, past them every condition drawn by its order (`past_targets`), as the PI ruled for a criterion on 2026-10-18; "N trials" counts a trial and its drummed repeats as one, an ordinary repeat a trial of its own (decision 31). Cost if wrong: one condition in `finished` (Appendix B, item 4).
5. **A plan's order is a declaration the scheduler plays** (scope call 5): `Fixed` (default), `Cyclic(cycles)` (`None`, until a time limit, a stop or the out-of-cage limit; warned at load), `Balanced(blocks)` (shuffled rounds, the last round partial where the count is not whole rounds, its types the seed's), `Random(blocks)` and `ByCriterion(fall_back)`. **A random order draws from a generator of its own**, `random.Random(f"blocks:{seed}")` from the run's recorded seed (scope call 22), so it replays and leaves every condition's draw as the seed gives it; `Fixed` draws nothing, so every seeded sequence the suite pins is unchanged. The run's sequence is kept by position (`_type_at`), drawn as far as it is read. **Never back-to-back** (answer 16): a shuffled round that would start with the type the last round ended with is turned by one -- still a round, drawing nothing more from the seed. With two types that makes a balanced order alternate after its first round (Appendix B, item 11). Cost: none found.
6. **Time limits are read in the run loop between trials, on the trial clock** (scope call 6; the code map's Surprise 7): `Session._elapsed` -- each trial's frames at the nominal frame period and its ITI -- which a pause does not move (Q6), not `Session.now()`, which an injected clock answers and which need not exclude a pause, and never `wall_now()`. Never in `_ends` or `_hold`, so no welfare-listed function changes and nothing acts during a pause. The block's limit is read first (`expire`), then the run's; **both wait for a drummed trial** (Q2; answer 15), the run's read only while none waits. The clock is nominal, unmeasured on a rig: a rig that drops frames runs longer than its limit says (XC-406, filed in Task 7; Appendix B, item 3). No block's start or end instant goes on the end row: `BLOCK_START` and `BLOCK_END` time them in the stream. Cost if wrong: the clock read differs by the time between trials.
7. **The per-advance progress check** (scope call 7, **changed**): the scope's "an advance with no trial drawn since the previous advance" would end a fixed plan whose middle block owes nothing. Built instead: **every block type the plan has was opened since the last trial and each ended before it drew one** (`Scheduler.stalled`), so no block it can open will draw; **a person's move starts the count afresh** (the engineering review, I1: moving back before a block's first trial made a plan whose other block could draw look stalled); and **a block that cannot draw and does not end** (`drawable`) ends the run too. Both with a sentence and `completed` (Q7), replacing the counted guard, which a cyclic or falling-back plan would trip by design. Cost if wrong: a plan that spins is ended later, never a working one sooner.
8. **A run a time limit or the progress check ends is `completed`, its end row saying why** (scope call 8, **changed** after the code map's Surprise 6): its own sentence says which, and **`end_reason`** -- `plan_done`, `plan_time`, `stalled`, `cannot_draw`; null for any other stop -- says it to a reader that must not parse the sentence (the science review, I3); never `limit`, which `/health` and the page read as the out-of-cage welfare stop. The scope proposed a new kind, `time`; `completed` needs no change to `health.py` or the page and is what a by-design end is. Cost if wrong: one value and two readers (Appendix B, item 10).
9. **What a timed-out block still owed is a `Shortfall`**, on the end row's `shortfalls`, **forgiving nothing**: the condition's counts still show it owed. `because` `TIME` for a block's own time limit, a top stage's included, and **`RUN_TIME` for the run's** (the science review, I3), so the record and the console say whose time ran out. Cost: none found.
10. **How each block ended goes on the end row, `block_ends`** (scope call 10): `ended` one of `rule`, `time`, `fall_back`, `next`, `back`, `run_time` or `run`, with who for a move and the last trial before it; **its `position` in the run's sequence and its `block_in_session`**, the number its `BLOCK_START` carried, null for a block that ran no trial and so was never opened in the stream (the engineering review, I4); and a top stage's **`passed_at`** (the science review, I1). **A block whose rule is met at the boundary the run's time runs out closes `rule`** (I3). Never a `runs.jsonl` row of its own. Cost: none found.
11. **`Service._start` changes in three lines** (scope call 11; Surprise 1): the plan into `RunSpec`, the plan into its own pre-flight (`_preflight`, not listed), and its docstring's sentence ("until either exists (XC-207) a run is one block") made true. A plan the task lacks fails the pre-flight's task checks item, so `preflight.gate` refuses the start unchanged and `_start` gains no branch. Cost: three edits to a listed function, Appendix A item 1.
12. **`CheckRun.plan` and `StartRun.plan` are optional, `None` the plain run** (scope call 12), decoded by `link._word(..., optional=True)`; `link._setting` untouched. **`StartRun.trials` stays required**: making it optional would change `_start` again (Surprise 3); a planned run ignores it and its start row records `trials` null, and the page hides the trials box while a plan is chosen, still sending its number. Cost: a wire field a planned run does not read.
13. **`wlx run --plan NAME`** (scope call 13): refused before the session opens and before the departure is asked, as a blocking finding is; `cli.main`'s `_marks.depart(session, departure)` line, `_settle_departure` and `_settle_return` untouched. Cost: none.
14. **Telemetry schema 17** (scope call 14): `Telemetry.plan`, `next_block`, `block_left_s`, `run_left_s`, `trials_to_run`, `passed_at` and `fell_back`; `Preflight.plan` and `plans`, the plans the pre-flight's task declares, which the page's picker offers -- so no task file is loaded on a housekeeping pass (`Service._tasks`; Surprise 2). The block's place in the sequence is not carried: its name and the next block's are. `wlx console` stays at XC-387. Cost: a console built for 16 refuses 17 by name, as every bump has.
15. **The load-time checks of plans, classed by TSK-51** (scope call 15, `check._plan_faults`; not `_block_faults`, the stimulus building blocks' -- Surprise 15). **Refused**, `bad-plan`, saying which plan and block and what to write: a plan of no blocks; a block's conditions not a list of `Condition`s; two block types of one plan, or two conditions of one block, with one name (XC-388); a block of no conditions ending on a criterion or a number of trials (XC-381); a condition playing a structure the task lacks, setting a setting the task does not declare (a bounded one -- a reward size -- among them), or setting it to a word where a number belongs or a choice not offered; a negative target; a criterion or fall-back rule outside (0, 1] or over a window under 1 (the `ZeroDivisionError`, Surprise 10); a number of trials, a count or a time limit that is no number of them; weights that are no weights. **Warned**, every kind: `late-condition` (XC-386); `endless-plan` (no end of its own and no time limit -- **a training progression's own end only where every stage has a time limit and no fall-back rule moves it back**, the science review's C3); `unbalanced-plan` (Q8); `empty-block` (over before it starts); `block-table-unread` (a block's own table naming `appears` or `withholding`, which only a structure says; Surprise 10); `no-run-limit` (`Constrained.max_run` under 1); **`weightless-condition`** (a condition of weight 0 owed trials, never drawn: the engineering review, I2); **`fall-back-unjudged`** (a fall-back on a plan of one stage, or at a stage that always ends before its window is in: its Minor 3); **`fall-back-bounce`** (a fall-back bar at or above a stage's pass bar, or a stage judging fewer answers than the fall-back: the science review, M2). **A condition's value outside its declared range is the pre-flight's to re-check widened** (`preflight.beyond`), not a load check: `beyond_range` runs `check_task` itself. Cost if wrong: a warning that should refuse lets a run fault, which the progress check then ends with a sentence.
16. **XC-380: `NO_LIMIT`** (scope call 16), the text `"no limit"`, which a block's own table gives as its `cap` to remove its task's limit; in a structure's table it reads as `None` does. Cost: a field value.
17. **Each condition plays its structure** (scope call 17): `Condition.structure`, the task's default when unset; the loop picks the `Trial` per draw (`make_world`, `run_trial`, `_appeared`), the scheduler the table per condition (`countings`; Surprise 11), five methods reading it. Cost: none on the frame path; the switch is between trials.
18. **The pre-flight covers the structures the plan plays** (scope call 18): a number one of the plan's conditions' structures uses and nothing gives fails, a number a condition sets counting as given (Surprise 9); each condition's values, over the starting values, re-checked widened (`beyond`). Cost: none.
19. **No reference task gets a plan** (scope call 19, **not adopted**): the tests write their own planned task files (`tests/_sessions.planned_task`), so no reference task's content hash moves and nothing reads as a real plan; real plans are the PI's, in wl-xtasks. Cost: none.
20. **Test the path, and fuzz** (scope call 20): a page `start` naming a plan through `Service._start`, its pre-flight and `Session.run` to the end row (Task 8); a scheduler fuzz of 400 random plans (Task 5) for spins, drums at a block's end, stages moved by two and balanced types back to back. The engineering review's session fuzz (`.superpowers/c2b-plan/work4/fuzz_sessions.py`, not part of the suite) is rerun on the build's tree with its controls (Task 12). Cost: none.
21. **The criterion window the console shows is the one the rules read** (scope call 21): unchanged from C2a.
22. **A random block order derives from the run's recorded seed** (scope call 22): call 5.
23. **XC-206 is not moved** (scope call 23): `BLOCK_END` at an advance still goes out at the boundary that advances. C2b makes advances common, but a hand move or a time limit is known only at that boundary, after its drain, so "right after the block's last `TRIAL_END`" cannot be known then. Cost: a pause held between two blocks is measured inside the earlier one, as today.
24. **The "until C2" comments** (scope call 24) are rewritten in the commits that make them false: `task.py`'s in Task 3, `simulate.py`'s and `taskd.py`'s structure line in Task 4, `scheduler.py`'s, `taskd._plan`'s, `SessionSpec.blocks`', `encode.py`'s and the event-encoding test's in Task 7, `service._start`'s and `preflight.py`'s in Task 8, `web.RUN_TRIALS`' in Task 10, `review.py`'s heading in Task 11.
25. **XC-207 closes** (scope call 25): a task declares its plans and the operator picks one at run start; a plan made at the rig beyond the task's own is the operator's edits, C3's (engine spec §13.2).
26. **A person's *next block* and *back one*** (Q10): *next* moves to the block the plan's order gives next, a training progression's next stage up; *back one* to the block before the open one in the run's sequence, started fresh, the plan carrying on from there (so the block left comes again). Both act at once at the boundary or while paused, **a drummed trial waiting dropped and named in the move's row** (`dropped`; the science review, M5), `BLOCK_END` closing a block that ran a trial; **the row names no event code** (the engineering review, C1: wl-preproc times the n-th row naming a code by the n-th strobe of it, and every block that ends on its own strobes `BLOCK_END` with no row); refused with a sentence in a plain run, past the plan's last block, before its first. Cost if wrong: a move that waits for the drum, one condition (Appendix B, item 9).
27. **A live edit wins over a value a condition sets** (engine spec §14.7; TSK-19), **following the spec against the code**: `taskd.py:2610` put a condition's values over every live edit (`{**self.spec.values, **condition.values}`; Surprise 9), harmless while the only condition set none. Now a setting applied live in a run (`_apply_staged`, `Session._edited`) wins over the condition's value for the rest of that run, setting it back not undoing that, and the trial's row lists it (`overridden`, §14.7's flag); **the edit's line on the changes feed names the conditions it overrides** (the science review, I5) until C3's console shows them. Cost if wrong: one dict order (Appendix B, item 1).
28. **The fall-back rule is the plan's, one for every stage** (Q3: "a second rule the plan states"), **judged on each trial's first answer, a broken hold the stage scores included** (answers 13 and 14), apart from the stage's own rule (the science review, C1); the first stage has none below it and does not fall back; a met rule counts before a fall-back met at the same answer -- **at the top stage for as long as its rule holds** -- and a fall-back before a stage's time. Cost if wrong: a rule per stage, one field (Appendix B, items 5 to 8).
29. **The record**: the start row gains `plan`, **`plan_form`** (the plan as declared, in the form the content hash reads it, so the record tells a balanced plan from a random one: the science review, I4) and, for a planned run, `trials` null and `blocks` the plan's block types **as declared** (not the run's sequence, which `blocks_run` and `block_ends` give: its M4); each trial line `structure` and `overridden`; `controls.jsonl` `next_block` and `back_block` rows; the end row `block_ends` (call 10) and `end_reason` (call 8). wl-preproc tolerates each (its readers read start and end rows by `.get` and a trial line's six fields), and Task 1 tells it before any is written. Cost: none found.
30. **The simulator keeps playing the default structure** (`simulate._structure`): a census per condition and block is C8's (engine spec §18.1). Cost: none.
31. **The console says each stage change, and a top stage passed** (the science review, I1 and I2): one line on the changes feed each time the plan moves to another block, with its reason and numbers ("two → one: fell back: 0 of its last 3 first answers right, under 50%"; `Scheduler.because`), a block the run ends with said by the run's stop reason; the plan row counts the fall-backs; a block past its targets says what it draws until -- its criterion, its number of trials, or both -- and a passed top stage "top stage passed at trial N; keeps working". Cost: none.
32. **A condition of weight 0 is never drawn** (the engineering review, I2): left out of the weighted draw, so a block whose every drawable condition weighs 0 has nothing to draw and the run ends with its sentence (`cannot_draw`), where `random.choices` raised; the draws of the rest are the ones the seed gave. Cost: none.

33. **Positions in a plan, since true visual angle merged** (`main` at `e988553`): a condition may set a whole-point setting to a `Polar` or a pair, held at load to that setting's choices as any choice is (`check._plan_faults`; a value off its choices refused, `bad-plan`), and set an eccentricity or a direction setting to a number as any number; the review report's plan tables show a condition's position eccentricity and direction first, its (x, y) beside them, as every position in the report is shown (`review._set_label`, `_point_label`; the PI, 2026-10-19, "Polar first, x-y allowed"). A setting's `Polar` value is recorded polar first wherever a value is written (Task 2; decision 17); C2b changes nothing else about positions. Cost: none.

## Global Constraints

- US English in code, comments and docs (CLAUDE.md).
- **The PI's decisions above**, each quoted where it is built; none re-asked.
- **Welfare** (Appendix A): the welfare-critical list is not widened and no limit is softened. The only listed function that changes is `Service._start` (three lines); `Session._command` gains two branches beside the pause, its `held` pass-through and its one `except` line unchanged. `Session._ends`, `_hold`, `_schedule`, `_manual_reward`, `Session.set`, `OUTSIDE_A_RUN`, `preflight.gate`, `out_of_cage`, `link._setting`, the four welfare modules and `cli.main`'s depart line are byte-identical; Task 12 compares them.
- **Time limits are read in the loop, never in `Session._ends`**, and never per frame (scope R25, R45).
- **wl-preproc's readers**: `runs.jsonl` holds start and end rows only; a trial line keeps the six required fields; one `block` string per block, its type; every block framed by `BLOCK_START`/`BLOCK_END` (neighbors §5). No new escape or marker.
- Hot path: **nothing new per frame**. A block's choice, its clock, the progress check, a condition's structure and a live edit's precedence are read or set between trials.
- No timing claim without a measurement (CLAUDE.md): a time limit is a declared duration, its clock nominal (XC-406); "one trial, five minutes at the worst" is `run.LONGEST_TRIAL_S`, arithmetic, not a measurement.
- No new dependency.
- Every new check and every fixed defect is proven able to fail (each task's **Step 6**, "Prove they can fail": a named break, its test seen failing as `N failed`, never an import error, the file restored from git). **Read the harness's output, not its exit code** (CLAUDE.md). Never run the suite, edit a test or `git add` while a sweep is in flight.
- **The interpreter**: the build worktree's own `.superpowers/venv/bin/python` (written `$PY`), set up as A2's was, **always with the shell's current directory at the build worktree** (`cd <worktree> && $PY …`), never with `-I`; check once that `$PY -c "import wl_xcon; print(wl_xcon.__file__)"` names the worktree's. `WLX_REQUIRE_PREPROC=1` works through the worktree's `wl-preproc` link (git ignores it).
- The suite: `WLX_REQUIRE_PREPROC=1 WLX_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1 PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 $PY -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -n auto`. One file: `$PY -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/<file>`. A red step's expected output is for its command as written. **Timing tests fail under heavy load**: believe a timing failure only if it fails alone at normal load.
- **How the code steps are written**: each edit is an exact text in a named file -- "replace ... with", "after ... add", "insert ... immediately before", "delete", "Create" -- every anchor occurring exactly once in its file when the edits before it in the step have been applied, top to bottom. Each fenced block is exact: blank lines it shows are part of it. **They were applied in the dry run by a script reading this file**, so an anchor that does not occur once is a sign the base moved: the controller fixes the anchor, never the code. New lines keep to 100 columns (XC-182's lint) but one: the page template's `plan-sel` label (Task 10), which mirrors the `task-sel` line beside it.
- **Every citation in a doc's prose names a key `docs/references/library.bib` has** (`tests/test_references.py`). This plan adds no key: `asaad2008flexible`, `nimhmonkeylogic2026task` and `berger2018standardized` are in the library, each cited only for what decisions.md's notes record it for; that `berger2018standardized` describes a progression stepping back is UNVERIFIED (its abstract, the only part read, does not say so; TSK-57).
- `YYYY-MM-DD` in a step is the day that step runs. **Backlog IDs**: XC-404 to XC-406 are this plan's, written as such; if `main`'s "Next free" has moved past XC-404 when the build starts, the controller renumbers them throughout before Task 1 (the test of the backlog's format fails on a number taken twice). **Decisions**: TSK-57 to TSK-63 are this plan's.

## Review Focus

Five inputs the spec implies and no task's own tests would otherwise exercise, each pinned by a test in its owning task:

1. **A plan's time limit falls due while the trainer has the run paused** (Q6; `_hold` welfare-listed and untouched). Expected: nothing ends during the pause; after the resume the run has its minutes of trials left, and ends at the first boundary after them, `completed`. Test: Task 7, `test_time_paused_does_not_count_against_a_plans_time_limit` (a pause three times the limit, then trials).
2. **A block's or a run's time runs out while a drummed trial is still coming back** (Q2; answer 15). Expected: neither ends until that trial is answered right; then the block ends `time` (or the run ends, `plan_time`), what it still owed recorded as not run. Tests: Task 5, `test_a_blocks_time_waits_for_a_drummed_trial_to_be_right`, `test_a_stage_whose_time_runs_out_moves_up_whether_or_not_it_passed`; Task 7, `test_a_runs_time_limit_finishes_a_drummed_trial_first`.
3. **A cyclic plan every one of whose blocks is over before it draws a trial** (Q7). Expected: the run ends with the sentence, `completed`, `end_reason` `stalled`, no trial run, the session still open -- never a spin with the animal in the chair. Tests: Task 5, `test_a_plan_that_has_opened_every_block_type_since_its_last_trial_has_stalled`; Task 7, `test_a_plan_that_can_draw_no_further_trial_ends_its_run_with_a_sentence` (its passes bounded, so a broken check faults rather than hangs) and `test_a_block_that_cannot_draw_and_does_not_end_ends_its_run_with_a_sentence`.
4. **A trainer pauses, presses *next block*, and resumes** (Q10). Expected: the move is applied while paused -- `_hold` drains it into `Session._command`, beside the pause, outside its listed parts -- recorded between the pause and the resume, and the first trial after the resume is the next block's. Test: Task 9, `test_a_move_sent_while_paused_is_applied_at_once`.
5. **A trainer edits live a value the condition sets** (engine spec §14.7). Expected: from the next boundary the edit wins over the condition's value for the rest of the run, the trial still counting toward its condition, each such trial's row names the value overridden, and the edit's feed line names the conditions. Tests: Task 4, `test_a_live_edit_wins_over_a_conditions_value_and_its_rows_say_so`, `test_a_live_edits_feed_line_names_the_conditions_it_now_overrides`.

### Task 1: Tell the neighbours, before any field is written

The lab's rule (`neighbors.md` §6; C2a's XC-365): tell a neighbour a field before writing it. **This task writes the asks first, before any task writes a field**, and the controller sends them to wl-preproc's session before the build merges: each run's plan on its start row, by name and **as declared** (`plan_form`; the science review, I4), a planned run's `trials` null and its `blocks` **the plan's block types as declared**, not the run's sequence (its M4); how each block ended on its end row (`block_ends`: `rule`, `time`, `fall_back`, `next`, `back`, `run_time` or `run`, with who, the last trial, **its `position` in the run's sequence and its `block_in_session` in the stream, null for a block that ran no trial** -- the engineering review, I4 -- and a top stage's `passed_at`); **why a completed run ended** (`end_reason`; the science review, I3); a timed-out block's shortfalls, `because` `time` or `run_time`; each trial's `structure` and `overridden`; and `controls.jsonl`'s `next_block` and `back_block`, with the drummed trial a move dropped (`dropped`) and **no event code** (the engineering review, C1: its `BLOCK_END` is timed by the stream, the row by its own instant); and that a plan's time limit ends a run `completed`, never `limit`. **And how a setting's value that is a polar position is written** (the PI, 2026-10-20, "Both, polar first", decision 17; Task 2 writes it): an object of its eccentricity and direction with its per-axis (x, y) beside them, a pair staying a two-item list -- told before it is written, beside the plan's fields, and not XC-399, each shown stimulus's true eccentricity and polar angle, which stays after C2b. wl-xtasks is told, as information, what the format gains (the PI's approval of this plan is the agreement, decision 36) and that **a plan is not a stage** (neighbors §1: XC-288's stages and C2b's plans overlap in meaning). wl-works needs nothing (neighbors §3). Backlog **XC-404**, in "Waiting on another repository", "Next free" raised to XC-405.

add

```markdown
# OPEN — engine build C2b: each run's plan, how each block ended, and each trial's structure

Opened YYYY-MM-DD by engine build C2b ([its plan](superpowers/plans/2026-10-19-engine-c2b.md), Task 1), backlog XC-404, **before any of these fields is written**: the build writes this first, and wl-preproc is told before the build merges. Read against wl-preproc's `wl_preproc/events/rigruns.py` (the `start`/`end` row filter and its `.get` reads), `rigtrials.py` (`_FIELDS`), `schema/core.py` (`stop_kind` stored as text up to 64 characters) and `schema/events.py` (a block's type kept only where every trial of it names one `block`), in wl-preproc's main at `b74d4f3` as read 2026-10-19.

## What changed on our side

**A run plays one of its task's plans** (the PI, 2026-10-07: "the operator chooses at run start among the plans it declares"), or the plain run of N trials as before. Each `runs.jsonl` **start** row gains `plan`, the plan's name in its task (whose content hash `versions.task` already carries), or null for the plain run, and `plan_form`, the plan as declared, in the form the content hash reads it (each declaration by its class's name and the fields it sets away from their defaults), so a reader can tell a balanced plan from a random one without the task file; its `blocks` lists the plan's block types **as declared**, in the plan's order -- not the run's sequence of blocks, which the end row's `blocks_run` and `block_ends` give -- and its `trials` is null for a planned run, whose lengths are its plan's. Each **end** row gains `block_ends`: a list, one per block the run opened, in order, of `{block, ended, by, after_trial, block_in_session, position, passed_at}` -- `ended` one of `rule` (its own end rule met), `time` (its own time limit), `fall_back` (a training progression's fall-back rule), `next` and `back` (a person moved the run on a block, or back one, `by` naming who), `run_time` (the run's own time limit ended the run with it open) or `run` (the run ended with it open otherwise: a stop, the out-of-cage limit, a fault, or the plan unable to go on); `after_trial`, the run's last trial before it ended, counted from 0 as `trial_index` is; `block_in_session`, the block's number in the session's stream, the one its `BLOCK_START` carries, **or null for a block that ran no trial**, which is neither opened nor closed in the stream and takes no number (a plan's block can be over before it draws one); `position`, its place in the run's sequence of blocks, from 0 -- a training progression's stage, which a fall-back or a person's *back one* moves back by one; and `passed_at`, for a training progression's top stage, where the animal keeps working once its rule is met, the trial after which that rule was first met, null otherwise -- since `BLOCK_END` carries no reason and the stream cannot. The end row gains `end_reason` too, beside `stop_kind` `completed`: `plan_done` (its plan's last block ended), `plan_time` (the plan's time limit), `stalled` (every block type the plan has was opened since its last trial and each ended before it drew one) or `cannot_draw` (a block that can draw nothing and does not end); null for a run that ended any other way. Its `shortfalls` gain `because` `time`, what a block still owed when its own time ran out, and `run_time`, what the open block still owed when the run's time ran out (the PI, 2026-10-19: "recorded as not run, 'time ran out'"), each forgiving nothing. A run that a plan's time limit ends, or whose plan can draw no further trial, ends `completed` with a sentence saying so -- **never `limit`, which stays the out-of-cage welfare stop's** (a plan's time limit is not a welfare limit). Each `trials.jsonl` line gains `structure`, the trial structure it played (its condition's, or the task's default), and `overridden`, the names of the values its condition set that a live edit replaced (engine spec §14.7; empty otherwise). `controls.jsonl` gains `next_block` and `back_block` rows (`block`, the block left; `to`, the one moved to; `dropped`, the condition whose drummed trial was waiting to come back and was dropped by the move, or null). **Their `event_code` is null**: a move closes a block that ran a trial with `BLOCK_END` (code 3), as an advance does, but every block that ends on its own strobes one with no row, so the n-th row naming 3 would not be the n-th 3 in the recording, and each move row is timed by its own `at`. **Each block still opens with `BLOCK_START` before its first trial and closes with `BLOCK_END` after its last, every trial of it naming one `block`, its type** -- a cyclic plan's second visit to a type is a new block with its own number -- so a block's type is kept as today. No new `runs.jsonl` row kind. **And a setting's value that is a polar position** (since true visual angle merged on 2026-10-20, a setting whose value is a whole position may offer `task.Polar` values, and a plan's condition may set one) **is recorded, wherever a value is written -- each `trials.jsonl` line's `params`, a start row's `layers` and `resolved`, a `parameter_changes.jsonl` row's `was` and `now` -- as an object: `{"eccentricity": 8.0, "direction": 45.0, "x": 5.675270513286652, "y": 5.675270513286651}`**, eccentricity and direction first, in degrees of visual angle, the direction counter-clockwise from rightward (engine spec §5.1), and beside them the per-axis degrees the screen is drawn in, converted exactly (`geometry.polar_deg`: `atan(tan e cos θ)` across and `atan(tan e sin θ)` up), so a reader that wants (x, y) has it without converting (the PI, 2026-10-20, "Both, polar first"). Its keys are `Polar`'s own field names and the (x, y) every per-axis position is written in. A value written as an (x, y) pair is a two-item list, as today, and every existing task writes its positions so. This is not XC-399 -- each shown stimulus's true eccentricity and polar angle on its trial line, which comes after C2b -- only how a setting that holds a polar position is written.

## The ask

**Read them into the published dataset**, so an analysis can tell which plan a run played and how it was declared, how each block ended (its rule, its time, a fall-back, a person's move, the run's time, or the run's end) and which block in the stream each entry is, why a completed run ended, what a timed-out block left unrun, which structure each trial played, and which trials ran with a value a live edit put over their condition's. Ask too, as wl-preproc's to decide: whether `plan` should be a column on `core.RunRecord`, a field on `ListedRun` and the NWB runs table (each `extra="forbid"`, so wl-works' validators must accept it before it appears), and whether the NWB `blocks` table should carry a block's end reason. Both readers tolerate the fields today (a start or end row is read with `.get`; a trial line's extra fields are ignored), so nothing breaks before this is done.

## What is deliberately not asked for

No new escape, code or marker: a plan's name, a block's end reason and a structure travel in the session folder, never the stream (wl-preproc's own rule, `contracts/events.py`: "parameter VALUES do not"). No change to the six required trial fields. **No condition numbers yet**: `CONDITION` (0x8003) stays unsent and the condition's name is in the trial line, until the lab's condition registry numbers conditions for good (the PI, 2026-10-19, "Wait for the registry"; XC-197; engine build C5).

---

```

### Task 2: A setting's polar value in the record, eccentricity and direction first

The PI's answer of 2026-10-20 (decision 17), "Both, polar first": "Recorded as eccentricity and direction with (x, y) beside, as you set for the record; wl-preproc told the shape before it is written. Fixed inside C2b's build, which rewrites these rows anyway, as its first code change." **The defect**, found rebuilding this plan on the true-angle merge (`e988553`) and older than C2b: since true visual angle merged, a setting whose value is a whole position may offer `task.Polar` values (`Param(..., choices=(Polar(8, 0), Polar(8, 45)))`, which check 8 measures), and a plan's condition may set one (Task 6 checks it, Task 7 plays it) -- but a `Polar` is not JSON, so **a run whose setting holds one faulted writing its start row** (`record.run_row`; a plain run and a plan's alike), and its trial rows' `params` and a live change's row would have faulted the same way. **Now every writer of a value in `record.py` writes a `Polar` through one function, `_written`** (`json.dumps`'s `default`): `{"eccentricity": 8.0, "direction": 45.0, "x": 5.675..., "y": 5.675...}`, eccentricity and direction as `Polar` holds them, in degrees of visual angle and counter-clockwise from rightward (engine spec §5.1), and beside them the per-axis (x, y) the screen is drawn in, converted exactly (`geometry.polar_deg`). **The keys** are `Polar`'s own field names -- so a reader rebuilds the value as the task wrote it, `Polar(row["eccentricity"], row["direction"])`, and the record says it in the vocabulary a task is written in -- and `x` and `y`, the names of the per-axis pair every other position is written as and the review report shows beside it ("(x, y)"); no `_deg` suffix, since every angle in the record is degrees. **A position written as a pair is a two-item list, as before**, so nothing changes for any existing task or for wl-preproc's reads of it; JSON's sorted keys put `direction` first on disk, which a reader of an object does not see. **The writers**: a run's start and end rows (`run_row`: `layers`, `resolved`), each trial's row (`trial`: `params`), a live change's row (`parameter_change`: `was`, `now`), `config.json` (`configure`) and a control's row (`control`), every one that can hold a setting's value; `warning`, `refusal` and the module's `welfare_note` write no value and are untouched. A `Polar` still holding a setting where a number belongs is no value, and `_written` raises naming it, as `json` would. **The readers** (each found by grepping `json.loads` and the record's file names across `wl_xcon` and `tools`): `resume.read` reads a stranded session's start rows' `bounded` values and run numbers, `parameter_changes.jsonl`'s `now` only for a welfare-bounded name or the out-of-cage limit, and `trials.jsonl`'s fluid, outcomes and drumming -- never a task setting's value, so it reads a record holding a polar value back unchanged, which a test pins; `stranded.find` reads `welfare_notes.jsonl` alone; no review, report or tool reads the record's values; the tests read the files as JSON. **The console's wire is untouched**: telemetry carries the run's own values, and a `Polar` reaches those only from code (a `SetParameter` from a console is refused by `link._setting` unless it is a number or a word, and a run's starting values travel the same way), never from a plan's condition; showing positions polar-first on the console is XC-401's. **Welfare**: `record.py` is on no list; `Session.set`, `_schedule`, `Service._start`, `stranded.py` and every other listed function are untouched (Task 12 compares them). The decision is REC-25 (STE-29 revised); Task 1's ask told wl-preproc the shape.

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/record.py`
- Modify (docs): `docs/references/decisions.md` (new REC-25; STE-29's revision)
- Modify (test): `tests/_sessions.py` (`polar_task`), `tests/test_record.py`, `tests/test_taskd.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `task.Polar`, `geometry.polar_deg` (on `main` since the true-angle merge).
- Produces: `record._written(value) -> dict`, the `default` of every writer of a value; `tests/_sessions.polar_task(folder, *, name="polar") -> Path`, the reference detection task with its target placed by one setting, `target_pos`, of two `Polar` choices, engine test material.

```python
def polar_task(folder: Path, *, name: str = "polar") -> Path:
    """The reference detection task with its target placed by one setting, `target_pos`, whose
    choices are polar positions -- 8° out to the right and 8° out at 45° (`task.Polar`; the PI,
    2026-10-19, "Polar first, x-y allowed") -- written into `folder` as `NAME.py`. Engine test
    material, run only in the simulator: no reference task has such a setting."""
    text = Path("tasks/fixation_detection.py").read_text()
    for old, new in (
        ('"target", at=(P("target_x"), P("target_y")), looks=P("target_appearance")',
         '"target", at=P("target_pos"), looks=P("target_appearance")'),
        ('            at=(P("target_x"), P("target_y")),\n            radius=P("target_window"),',
         '            at=P("target_pos"),\n            radius=P("target_window"),'),
        ('        Param("target_x", unit="deg", low=-16.0, high=16.0, start=10.0),',
         '        Param("target_pos", unit="deg", choices=(Polar(8.0, 0.0), Polar(8.0, 45.0))),\n'
         '        Param("target_x", unit="deg", low=-16.0, high=16.0, start=10.0),'),
        ("from wl_xcon.task import (", "from wl_xcon.task import (\n    Polar,"),
    ):
        assert text.count(old) == 1, old
        text = text.replace(old, new)
    text += (
        "\n\nimport dataclasses as _polar_dataclasses\n"
        "from wl_xcon.task import NOT_DIFFICULTY, STIMULUS, Meaning\n"
        "TASK = _polar_dataclasses.replace(TASK, meanings={\n"
        "    **TASK.meanings,\n"
        "    'target_pos': Meaning(NOT_DIFFICULTY, STIMULUS, note='the target position'),\n"
        "})\n"
    )
    path = Path(folder) / f"{name}.py"
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text(text)
    return path

```

```python
def test_a_polar_value_is_written_as_its_eccentricity_and_direction_with_its_x_y_beside(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-20, "Both, polar first": a setting's value that is a polar position
    (`task.Polar`) is recorded as its eccentricity and direction, with the per-axis (x, y) the
    screen is drawn in beside them, converted exactly (`geometry.polar_deg`) -- a `Polar` is not
    JSON, and a run of one faulted writing its start row. A pair is written as it always was, a
    list; a `Polar` holding a setting where a number belongs is no value, and is refused by name."""
    from wl_xcon.geometry import polar_deg
    from wl_xcon.task import P, Polar

    record = SessionRecord.open(tmp_path, session_id="2027-01-14_01", subject="A")
    record.trial(
        index=1, outcome="correct", params={"pos": Polar(8.0, 45.0), "pair": (5.0, 0.0)}, run=0,
        position=Position.lone(1), fluid_ml=0.0, last_reward_at=None,
    )
    (row,) = [json.loads(line) for line in (
        tmp_path / "2027-01-14_01" / "xcon" / "trials.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()]
    x, y = polar_deg(8.0, 45.0)

    assert row["params"] == {"pos": {"eccentricity": 8.0, "direction": 45.0, "x": x, "y": y},
                             "pair": [5.0, 0.0]}
    with pytest.raises(TypeError, match="Polar"):
        record.trial(
            index=2, outcome="correct", params={"pos": Polar(P("ecc"), 45.0)}, run=0,
            position=Position.lone(2), fluid_ml=0.0, last_reward_at=None,
        )

```

```python
def test_a_settings_polar_value_is_recorded_wherever_a_value_is_and_reads_back(tmp_path):
    """The PI, 2026-10-20, "Both, polar first": a run whose setting holds a polar position
    (`task.Polar`, since true visual angle merged) writes it wherever a value is written -- its
    start row's layers and resolved values, each trial row's `params`, a live change's
    `parameter_changes.jsonl` row -- as its eccentricity and direction with its (x, y) beside them,
    where the run faulted writing its start row, a `Polar` not being JSON. A resume reads the
    record back as it reads any other."""
    from wl_xcon import resume
    from wl_xcon.geometry import polar_deg
    from wl_xcon.task import Polar

    path = _sessions.polar_task(tmp_path)
    link = _Drained({2: [SetParameter(name="target_pos", value=Polar(8.0, 45.0), by=Box("jake"))]})
    session = _session(_spec(tmp_path, task=str(path), trials=4, values={
        **_spec(tmp_path).values, "target_pos": Polar(8.0, 0.0)}), link=link)

    session.run()

    x, y = polar_deg(8.0, 45.0)
    right = {"eccentricity": 8.0, "direction": 0.0, "x": 8.0, "y": 0.0}
    oblique = {"eccentricity": 8.0, "direction": 45.0, "x": x, "y": y}
    start, _ = _runs(session)
    assert start["resolved"]["target_pos"] == start["layers"]["run"]["target_pos"] == right
    placed = [row["params"]["target_pos"] for row in _trial_rows(session)]
    assert placed[0] == right and placed[-1] == oblique
    assert all(value in (right, oblique) for value in placed)
    ((was, now),) = [(row["was"], row["now"]) for row in _parameter_changes(session)]
    assert (was, now) == (right, oblique)
    assert resume.read(session.directory, session.welfare.left_cage_wall_at).run_index == 0

```

```python
def _written(value: object) -> object:
    """**A value `json` cannot write, as the record writes it** (`json.dumps`'s `default`): a
    setting's value that is a polar position, a `task.Polar` -- since true visual angle merged, a
    setting whose value is a whole position may offer `Polar` values, and a plan's condition may
    set one -- **recorded as its eccentricity and direction, with the per-axis (x, y) the screen
    is drawn in beside them** (the PI, 2026-10-20, "Both, polar first": "Recorded as eccentricity
    and direction with (x, y) beside"): `{"eccentricity": 8.0, "direction": 45.0, "x": 5.675...,
    "y": 5.675...}`, (x, y) converted exactly (`geometry.polar_deg`), so a reader that wants either
    has it without converting. The keys are `Polar`'s own field names, which a reader rebuilds it
    from, and the (x, y) every per-axis position is written in. A position written as a pair is a
    two-item list, as it always was. **Anything else raises `TypeError`**, as `json` does: a
    `Polar` holding a setting is no value, and a record never writes a stand-in for one."""
    from wl_xcon.geometry import polar_deg
    from wl_xcon.task import Polar

    def number(field: object) -> bool:
        return isinstance(field, (int, float)) and not isinstance(field, bool)

    if isinstance(value, Polar) and number(value.eccentricity) and number(value.direction):
        x, y = polar_deg(float(value.eccentricity), float(value.direction))
        return {"eccentricity": value.eccentricity, "direction": value.direction, "x": x, "y": y}
    raise TypeError(f"the record writes no {type(value).__name__} value ({value!r})")


```

```markdown
- **Basis:** PI decision, under STE-27. The conversion is geometry (our arithmetic): the direction (sin e cos θ, sin e sin θ, cos e) in per-axis degrees. The name `Polar` and its fields are an engineering default, the fields in the PI's words. No reference task's position was converted: `adaptive_detection`'s target is placed by `target_x` and `target_y` in a trial that is `fixation_detection`'s unchanged by design, and its `target_eccentricity` is read by nothing until a between-trial procedure (XC-242), as the PI confirmed its meaning (TRN-10); changing that is his. The console and the record show eccentricity and direction after engine build C2b, which rewrites them: the console as XC-401, the record (each stimulus's true eccentricity and direction beside its (x, y)) as XC-399. No published source was sought.
```

add

```markdown
### REC-25 — A setting's value that is a polar position is recorded as its eccentricity and direction with its per-axis (x, y) beside them, wherever a value is written; a pair stays a two-item list
- **Decided:** 2026-10-20, the PI, asked how a run whose setting holds a `task.Polar` should be recorded (it faulted writing its start row, a `Polar` not being JSON; found rebuilding the engine C2b plan on the true-angle merge): "Both, polar first" -- "Recorded as eccentricity and direction with (x, y) beside, as you set for the record; wl-preproc told the shape before it is written. Fixed inside C2b's build, which rewrites these rows anyway, as its first code change." The keys, `Polar`'s own field names `eccentricity` and `direction` with `x` and `y`, are engineering ([the engine C2b plan](../superpowers/plans/2026-10-19-engine-c2b.md), Task 2).
- **Where:** [`wl_xcon/record.py`](../../wl_xcon/record.py) (`_written`, every writer of a value: a run's start row, each trial's `params`, a `parameter_changes.jsonl` row, `config.json`, a control row); STE-29; the ask to wl-preproc in [pending-wl-preproc-amendments.md](../pending-wl-preproc-amendments.md#open--engine-build-c2b-each-runs-plan-how-each-block-ended-and-each-trials-structure).
- **Basis:** PI decision; the conversion is STE-29's (`geometry.polar_deg`), exact. No published source.

```

---

### Task 3: The plan vocabulary in the task object

A task's plans live in its task object (decision 18; engine spec §13.1). **The block vocabulary moves into `task.py`** (call 1) -- `Order`, `Shuffled`, `WithReplacement`, `Constrained`, `Condition` and `Block`, from `scheduler.py`, which imports them under the same names -- because `task.py` cannot import `scheduler.py` (a cycle) and the format pins read the task's own vocabulary (`tests/test_declarations.py`'s `_vocabulary`, by module; Surprise 12). Beside them: **`Condition.structure`** (decision 22; played in Task 4), **`Block.trials`** and **`Block.time_limit_s`** (decision 20; read in Tasks 5 and 7), the block orders **`Fixed`**, **`Cyclic`**, **`Balanced`** (never back-to-back where rounds meet, answer 16), **`Random`** and **`ByCriterion`** (its fall-back judged on each trial's first answer, a broken hold the stage scores included, answers 13 and 14) (decisions 19, 23 and 24; the PI's answers Q3, Q8, Q8b; each played in Task 5), **`Plan`** with its run time limit (Q5) and **`Task.plans`** (call 3). Every field at a default, so every task's content hash is as it was and `FORMAT` stays 1; `DEFAULTS` gains each class. **And XC-380**: `NO_LIMIT`, which a block's own table gives as its `cap` to remove its task's repeat limit, where `None` keeps it (decision 30; call 16) -- read by `Scheduler._cap` and the review report. The comments that said the default structure plays "until plans exist (engine build C2)" are made true (call 24).

**Files:**
- Modify: `wl_xcon/task.py`, `wl_xcon/scheduler.py`, `wl_xcon/review.py`
- Modify (docs): `docs/design/architecture.md`, the engine spec (§3.5's note), `docs/references/decisions.md` (TSK-50), `docs/backlog.md` (XC-380's line goes)
- Modify (test): `tests/test_declarations.py`, `tests/test_task_object.py`, `tests/test_counting.py`, `tests/test_scheduler.py`, `tests/test_review.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `task.Counting`, `task.Trial` (unchanged).
- Produces: `task.NO_LIMIT = "no limit"`; `Counting(cap=NO_LIMIT)`; `task.Order`, `Shuffled`, `WithReplacement(weights)`, `Constrained(max_run, weights)`, `Condition(name, values, target, structure=None)`, `Block(name, conditions, criterion=None, counting=None, order=Shuffled(), drum=False, trials=None, time_limit_s=None)`; `BlockOrder`, `Fixed()`, `Cyclic(cycles=None)`, `Balanced(blocks=None)`, `Random(blocks=None)`, `ByCriterion(fall_back=None)`; `Plan(blocks, order=Fixed(), time_limit_s=None)`, refused as built when malformed in shape; `Task.plans: dict[str, Plan] = {}`, refused as built when malformed in shape. `scheduler.Block` and the rest are the same objects.

```python
def test_a_plan_hashes_the_same_in_another_process(tmp_path):
    """Engine build C2b: a plan's blocks, weights and orders are declarations the hash writes field
    by field, none of them a set or an address, so a task with plans hashes alike in every
    process."""
    path = tmp_path / "planned_task.py"
    path.write_text(PLANNED_FILE)

    def elsewhere(seed: str) -> str:
        done = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, "-c", HASH_IT.replace("module.TASK", "module.PLANNED"), str(path)],
            cwd=ROOT, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONHASHSEED": seed}, capture_output=True, text=True,
            timeout=120,
        )
        assert done.returncode == 0, done.stderr
        return done.stdout.strip()

    assert elsewhere("1") == elsewhere("999") == content_hash(_module(path).PLANNED)


```

```python
def test_a_tasks_plans_are_part_of_its_content():
    """Engine build C2b: which blocks a run can play, how they follow, what each condition sets and
    plays, and when a block or a run is over change what a run of the task is, so each moves the
    hash; a task with no plans hashes as before the field existed."""
    plain = _task(HOLD)
    near = Condition("near", {"fix_hold": 0.3}, target=10)
    block = Block("hold", [near])

    def of(**plans):
        return content_hash(Task(plain.structures, default=plain.default, plans=plans))

    variants = {
        of(),
        of(one=Plan([block])),
        of(two=Plan([block])),
        of(one=Plan([block], order=Cyclic())),
        of(one=Plan([block], order=Cyclic(cycles=3))),
        of(one=Plan([block], order=ByCriterion(fall_back=(0.5, 20)))),
        of(one=Plan([block], time_limit_s=600.0)),
        of(one=Plan([dataclasses.replace(block, time_limit_s=60.0)])),
        of(one=Plan([dataclasses.replace(block, trials=40)])),
        of(one=Plan([Block("hold", [dataclasses.replace(near, structure="wait")])])),
        of(one=Plan([dataclasses.replace(
            block, counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=NO_LIMIT))])),
    }
    assert len(variants) == 11
    assert of() == content_hash(plain)


```

```python
def test_a_tables_no_limit_is_shown_as_no_limit():
    """XC-380 (engine build C2b): `NO_LIMIT` reads as no repeat limit, never as a number."""
    table = Counting(after_stimulus=SPENT, appears="hold", cap=NO_LIMIT)
    holding = dataclasses.replace(HOLDING, counting=table)
    out = render_task(_reviewed(structures={"holding": holding}))

    assert "no repeat limit: a condition is repeated until done" in out
    assert "a repeat limit of no limit" not in out
```

```python
def test_a_blocks_no_limit_removes_its_tasks_repeat_limit():
    """XC-380 (engine build C2b): a block's own table says "no limit" with `NO_LIMIT`, where `None`
    keeps the task's -- so a hold-training block can repeat a trial until it is done though its
    task moves a condition past at its second failure in a row. In a structure's own table the two
    say the same."""
    def moved_past(block_cap, structure_cap=1) -> list:
        table = Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=block_cap)
        block = Block(name="b", conditions=[Condition("a", {}, target=3)], counting=table)
        scheduler = Scheduler([block], seed=1,
                              counting=Counting(after_stimulus=OWED, cap=structure_cap))
        for _ in range(6):
            if scheduler.finished:
                break
            scheduler.record(scheduler.next_trial().name, Outcome.FIXATION_BREAK)
        return [short.after_trial for short in scheduler.shortfalls]

    assert moved_past(None) == [1]
    assert moved_past(NO_LIMIT) == []
    assert moved_past(None, structure_cap=NO_LIMIT) == []


```

```python
@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("plans", "said"),
    [
        ([("hold", "a plan")], "a dict of plan names to Plans"),
        ({"": "a plan"}, "named by text"),
        ({"hold": "a plan"}, "is a Plan, not str"),
    ],
)
def test_a_tasks_plans_are_refused_as_built_when_malformed(plans, said):
    """Engine build C2b: a task's plans are a dict of names to `Plan`s, refused as built otherwise,
    as its structures are."""
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=said):
        Task({"a": _trial()}, default="a", plans=plans)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("fields", "said"),
    [
        ({"blocks": (Block("b", []),)}, "a list of Blocks"),
        ({"blocks": [Block("b", []), "b"]}, "a list of Blocks"),
        ({"blocks": [], "order": "fixed"}, "a BlockOrder"),
        ({"blocks": [], "time_limit_s": "10 minutes"}, "a number of seconds"),
        ({"blocks": [], "time_limit_s": True}, "a number of seconds"),
    ],
)
def test_a_plan_is_refused_as_built_when_malformed(fields, said):
    """Its shape as it is built; what it declares -- an empty plan, a limit of no time -- at load
    (`check.check_task`)."""
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=said):
        Plan(**fields)


def test_the_block_vocabulary_is_the_tasks_and_the_scheduler_plays_it():
    """Engine build C2b: a plan's blocks are declared in the task object, so `Block`, `Condition`
    and the orders are the task's vocabulary, the classes the format pins hold
    (`tests/test_declarations.py`); the scheduler imports the same classes under its old names."""
    for name in ("Order", "Shuffled", "WithReplacement", "Constrained", "Condition", "Block"):
        assert getattr(scheduler, name) is getattr(vocabulary, name)
        assert getattr(vocabulary, name).__module__ == "wl_xcon.task"
    plan = Plan([Block("b", [Condition("c", {}, target=1, structure="a")])])
    task = Task({"a": _trial()}, default="a", plans={"p": plan})
    assert task.plans["p"].blocks[0].conditions[0].structure == "a"
    assert Task({"a": _trial()}, default="a").plans == {}
```

`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`: delete

```python


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Order:
    """How the next condition is chosen. **Declared per block** (PI, 2026-08-31)."""


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Shuffled(Order):
    """Every still-owed condition once per pass, in random order.

    Balance as the block progresses, and a bound on how long an animal waits for any
    condition. Partly predictable near the end of a pass, when few remain.
    """


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class WithReplacement(Order):
    """Each trial drawn independently, optionally weighted.

    No sequential predictability, and it supports conditions wanted rarely -- catch
    trials, probes. Balance only in expectation, so the counters matter more.
    """

    weights: dict = field(default_factory=dict)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Constrained(Order):
    """Drawn with replacement, refusing runs longer than `max_run`.

    What most search and attention designs actually use, because an animal exploits
    runs: three of the same target position in a row and it stops searching and
    starts predicting.
    """

    max_run: int = 2
    weights: dict = field(default_factory=dict)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Condition:
    name: str
    values: dict
    target: int
```

`wl_xcon/scheduler.py`: delete

```python


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class Block:
    name: str
    conditions: list[Condition]
    #: `(proportion, window)` -- "80% correct over the last 20 answers", a percentage; a proportion
    #: of 1.0 is a streak, "N correct in a row" (the engine C2a plan, revised 2026-10-18). Judged on
    #: every answer, and on a broken hold or a failure to acquire fixation where this block's own
    #: table scores it (`Scheduler._scored`); a percentage judges only each trial's first answer
    #: under `first_answers`, a streak never. (`Scheduler.record`.) The block ends once every
    #: condition is owed nothing and, with a criterion, the criterion is met too: **its targets are
    #: a minimum** (`Scheduler.finished`).
    criterion: tuple[float, int] | None = None
```
